“Undoubtedly, Your Majesty! A true find! A success for our expedition in Your Majesty's name and honor! Soon we'll announce it to the world. Through your journal, of course, if that would please Your Majesty”.

“You even have to ask? Make the announcement – today! Consider my journal at your full disposal, it's yours as completely as your own head”.

“Thank you, our deepest wholehearted gratitude from the bottom of our souls, Your Majesty! Such a great help – thank you a thousand times over!” The pudgy man was stirred nearly to tears. “The most highly educated and culturally advanced public will never forget your greatest deeds and immeasurable generosity, Your Majesty!”

“And the dances?”

“Oh, yes! Of course, the dances!” Asaol perked up. “It's barely believable! These tribes! In the Eastern Winds! Oh! Your Majesty! I brought back eight and a half dances of the Anaa people, especially from Lake Aenn! Oh!”

“Eight? Incredible!”

“Nooo, Your Majesty, allow me to clarify – not eight, but eight and a half, Your Majesty!” practically sang out the elated puffball in operatic style.

“A marvelous stroke of luck!” the Queen was so overcome with joy and happiness that she kept darkening and now was far beyond human visibility – Rina could no longer distinguish Her Majesty's brows or lips – only the Queen's nose somehow, miraculously, had not yet vanished into the total blackness of the royal cheeks.

“And one hundred thirty-three cloudographs from the same districts!” Asaol declared triumphantly, almost sending the Queen into another trance.

Without waiting for praise or questions, he creakily moved toward his mega-bag, deftly unbuckled its many straps, and, his face radiating pride, drew from its depths five or six gray cloth scraps – ultra-thin shreds of not-yet-solidified mist. The Queen even clapped her hands.

“Here they are, just a moment, Your Majesty”, the big man muttered, lovingly smoothing the trimmings over his barrel-shaped calves. “These aren't all, Your Majesty – just a portion, a small one. The rest are in the Ice Laboratory. But these cloudographs – they're true masterpieces, true ones! Made by my student Agaya”.

Then the magic happened: Asaol took one of the shreds, pulled it at both ends, and blew a whistling jet onto it. The crumpled fragment swiftly transformed into a snowball – but in a moment changed shape, and... revealed itself to the stunned eyes of the audience as a wondrously beautiful model – a multicolored, over-delicate mold of peaks, valleys, sky-rivers, and jagged shores of a distant southern archipelago. A miniature mountain glistened with silvery glaciers, in a tiny river, blue waves of the purest air played – and most astonishing of all, a soft, furry cloud hovered above the summit. This whole mirage rested on Asaol's fleshy palm and was, clearly, weightless.

“But surely places like that have been settled properly by our people by now?” the Queen asked, finally broking the silence, having soaked in the fascinating beauty of the magical island replica.

“Settled?! Not at all!” Asaol retorted joyfully. “Not even a post office there!”

“Unbelievable! Then what's the administration doing?”

“Watching a puddle!” Asaol exclaimed.

The Queen turned her coal-black eyes on him and suddenly exploded into thunderous laughter. Asaol instantly picked up on her merry spirits, letting out fifty rapid giggles. The island model began to wobble dangerously on his hand... and fell! Flew downward! Rina instinctively lunged forward and, like a seasoned volleyball player, struck the island with the back of her hand. It bounced like a ball, then shriveled, lost its shape before their eyes, and, once again as a gray scrap, drifted softly down to the marble like a fallen maple leaf.

The Queen and Asaol stopped laughing at once.

“Meet our guest”, said the Queen with sudden severity. “She's from Earth. Ahaa found her not far from the border of the Aisa Province”.

“Rina”, Rina said with a bow.

“Asaol. V-very pleased, at your s-service”, he stammered, arching his eyebrows and examining Rina as if she hadn't been sitting beside him all this time but had just dropped onto his head from the ceiling.

“Rina is traveling through the World of Air, but prefers our kingdom above all others, and especially the royal upper society”, Queen Aoeina the Third added in the same stern tone.

“Not surprising, Your Majesty!” Asaol turned to her excitedly. “No other kingdom can even dream of the true elevation, purity, and coolness that our realm has in such abundance!”

“Listen!” The Queen suddenly had a wonderful idea. “Do you have your clouparatus with you?”

“As always, my Great Glorious Lady! For a scientist and decent cloudographer such as myself – if I may say so – it would be unforgivable to go even a minute without a clouparatus. One might miss something important!”

“Then I want to be cloudographed right now!”

“With the greatest pleasure, Your Majesty”.

“No, I have changed my mind”, Aoeina reconsidered. “Better yet – let us cloudograph our guest”.

“Me?” Rina asked, surprised.

“Yes, you, of course you!” Asaol jumped. “Definitely you – and right away!”

“On the Throne!” the Queen commanded.

“As you wish, Your Majesty!” While remaining bowed, the famous cloudographer swiftly and efficiently assembled the pieces of his clouparatus.

Eventually, Rina was seated again on that thoroughly iced-over cube, on the Throne itself, and cloudographed properly as planned. The clouparatus, oddly enough, looked very much like a white Bosch coffee maker. And just a minute later, Rina was handed a gray scrap of cloth.

“Blow!” Asaol requested, beaming with boundless joy.

Rina blew, the cloth trembled, quickly rounded out, and then, in the next few moments, it turned into... Rina, sitting on the Throne, absolutely lifelike, though shrunk to about one-twelfth of her true size.

And five minutes later, the court cloudographer and brave explorer of the far reaches of the Cloud Kingdom hastily departed, citing an urgent report to the Society of High Air Currents. The Queen let Asaol go with some reluctance, though only after extracting a firm promise that he'd return tomorrow morning with all his travel trophies straight to the Second Vestibule at the door-opening hour. Asaol, nearly in tears and bowing non-stop, guaranteed he'd be there on time – even if the sky fell on the sun or the sun fell on the sky. Rina was no longer listening.

“An extraordinary scientist”, the Queen said with feeling once he'd disappeared behind the columns. “And a fine citizen, wouldn't you agree?”

“I do”. Rina nodded.

The Queen stood up and strode quickly toward the exit.

“Alla righ, I hear ya. I'll set ya uppa. You'll see da Fountain”, she said, temporarily slipping deeper into the old street speech as Rina finally caught up. “T'be fair, we notta share secrets with drifters. But since ya pal Gerks...”

“Herx”, Rina corrected.

“All the more so! Since your friend knows everything about the Fountain – as you claim – you mean he knows it down to the smallest crack, then whether or not you see the Fountain doesn't change a thing. I'm certain we lose nothing in terms of stability or traditions of the monarchy. Arr ya with?”

“I am, Your Majesty”.

The Queen surged forward quite decisively but after about twenty steps came to a thoughtful halt.

“Would it trouble you to exit with me not through the Eastern passage but the Western one?” she asked. “You see, I assured the special curator Aavis that I'd make arrangements for additional funds for Aaolis's water research. Making arrangements is simple, although, as you understand, it's wiser to take a look at the research with one's own eyes first. And since we're already heading to the lower levels, it would be downright foolish not to stop by the Steam Laboratory”.

And, as usual, without waiting for any response or agreement, Aoeina the Third stomped barefoot across the snowy-white tiles with firm, ringing steps, completely unbothered by her barefootedness. And, picking up speed, she flew out from under the arches of the Throne Hall like a whirlwind, dashing down the side corridor...

25 – THE MORNING EVENING
part four

The Laboratory of Vapors and Steams was conveniently situated just past the last hall right on the Eighth Eastern Staircase. It's worth noting that the Pink Palace consisted mostly of halls immeasurable in all directions – ballrooms, ceremonial halls, colonnaded spaces, and all sorts of other grand chambers – and at the same time, of tiny, pitiful little rooms, cramped even for one person. So, to accommodate offices, departments, reception areas, administrations, and especially laboratories, the Senior Palace Master made use of every conceivable crevice, including underutilized staircases.

For example, a year ago, by his order, the Eighth Eastern Staircase was covered with a light roof, blocked off with two walls, with windows added to both sides, and thus, through a simple procedure, with minimal time and effort, the staircase was transformed into a perfect research center for all manner of watery and vapor-related studies.

True, the staff of this temple of science grew very tired running up and down the steps, but on the other hand, the issue of hierarchy was logically resolved, the higher the rank and authority, the higher – literally – the workplace was located.

As a result, on the first two steps, jammed into a miserable little area, labored sixteen MoJuStas (Most Junior Staff) while the upper platform, about half the size of a tennis court, was firmly occupied by a single though highly distinguished head of the entire operation. To reach him, scientists had to break through layers of steel-barricaded bureaucracy of all kinds. Only a handful of people, the Queen included, had keys to the barely visible door that allowed them to step straight onto the upper platform from the outside.

“And what do they study in the Steam Laboratory?” Rina asked.

“Steam, of course. Though I think they haven't studied it in a long time – they make it, or rather try to produce it”, replied the Queen.

“But steam is produced without any laboratory at all – I think it's a very simple thing”, said Rina, clearly puzzled.

“Of course, it's simple. Nothing's easier than making regular steam – cold or even hot – though it's not cheap. But our pioneers dry it”.

Rina raised her eyebrows.

“Wait, you'll see soon enough”. The Queen smiled.

The lab was buzzing with the utmost activity – if not outright merriment. The leadership had gone off somewhere, and the lower lab staff were amusing themselves by holding a stair-running race with glass flasks on their heads. They simply stuck their skulls into large chemical vessels and ran up and down the steps like that, pink tongues hanging out. The goal of the contest wasn't speed – it was to fog up the inside of the flasks so thoroughly with such heavy breathing that their faces couldn't be seen by the judges. After all, certified steam specialists could only be thrilled by a steam game.

The Queen's sudden appearance struck the lab crew with such staggering force that four of them tumbled headlong onto the steps at full tilt, clattering outrageously with their unbreakable glassware. Who knows how the untimely game might have ended, if not for the Second Supervisor Sub, who happened to be lingering with a report and hadn't gone home with the other bosses.

“In defense of the state and the Great Queen!” he bellowed at the top of his lungs, springing like a coil from his mid-upper platform straight to Her Majesty's feet.

“Where's Aaolis?” the Queen asked indifferently.

“The General Director is in an urgent meeting”, the Sub lied automatically.

“I wish to review your work now and at once”, the Queen frowned in displeasure. “My time is expensive, and my patience even more so. And frankly, I don't understand how some minor official dares delay me here, let alone...”

“I most humbly beg...” groaned the Sub.

“What is it?” the Queen gave her fingers a disdainful flick.

“I most humbly implore Your Kind and Just Majesty to allow me, as one of the seniors in the lab, to present to Your Majesty the freshest, most magnificent achievements of our marvelous science center”.

Looking at the Sub, Rina thought he probably didn't have a single bone in his body. He writhed like a rubber snake. His head seemed unattached to his neck, and each of his legs appeared to have not one but eight knees.

The Queen sighed and turned to Rina:

“I don't think they're able to show us everything without the manager, but of course we won't wait for their foolish boss to return from his idiotic meeting. I'll send my inspector here a bit later, and if this so-called General Director still hasn't returned, I'll banish him to the Aot Islands for life, along with his family, relatives, and neighbors!”

“Fair! Indisputably fair!” muttered the Sub, both sincerely and out of flattery.

“Show us!” the Queen barked.

The Sub disappeared as if he were blasted away with a massive kick, but within just moments he returned with three of the steam lab's top specialists. Each carried two flasks – the same ones they'd just had on their heads – now filled with suspicious-looking liquids.

“If it would please the Great, Wise, and Most Beautiful...” the Sub began to plead.

“Brief!” the Queen cut in impatiently.

“Yes, of course! Very briefly!” the Sub pointed at the specialists with a ruler, as if they were exhibits in a wax museum. “Here you see six stages of the main research – the six, if I may say so, leaps toward triumph. The first is water. Note, completely transparent water in every respect, Your Majesty. The second leap – also water, but murky. The third...” the Sub stepped toward the next scientist, “a major discovery! Meltwater, indistinguishable from that obtained by melting snowflakes, but produced without snow! The superlative scientific breakthrough! Though we have even more impressive results.

“Please turn your attention to the fourth vessel, Your Majesty, here you behold water extracted from an artificial cloud grown chemically at the testing range named after the great and unforgettable ae-aet. The fifth vessel – almost the same water, but not extracted, rather punched out with big special sticks”.

The Sub was clearly out of breath and switched rapidly to a conspiratorial whisper, “I dare remind Your Majesty that our Director nearly died during the extraction, having been struck accidentally in the forehead by his own cloud-beater. But it was precisely this blow that inspired the brilliant idea of extracting water from non-resilient clouds, which we subjected to cooling via a unique proprietary method – we keep it confidential as a state secret”.

“And finally, the highest scientific pinnacle – water from air, obtained without clouds whatsoever. An extremely promising discovery, Your Majesty! To date, no one has succeeded in extracting ordinary water from regular air. Our Laboratory is one of a kind in every respect!”

“And where are the dried water and dry steam?” the Queen asked indignantly. “Don't tell me that in an entire year, you still haven't managed to produce a single handful of powdered water?!”

“We're on the brink of success, Your Majesty!” The Sub was nearly leaping out of his own skin. “Just two steps away from a full one-hundred-percent victory! A few more stages, five or seven more discoveries, and we'll obtain not only dry water but – I'm convinced – every kind of water too!”

“What do you mean, every kind?” asked the Queen, slightly disappointed.

“F-flam-mable, for ins-s-stance!” the Sub hissed in a whistling whisper.

“Flammable?”

“Exactly, Your Majesty! Inflammably-ignitable, firing combustibly-burnable! The water that flames and fires like a lightning strike or stronger – destructive and exclusively hot, Your Majesty!”

The Queen paused in disbelief, her mouth open, her eyes huge.

“There… there might be something to that”, she said thoughtfully, relaxing for the first time since arriving at the Steam Laboratory. “I think combustible water might turn out to be even more important than the dried kind... Don't you believe we need to restructure the lab and change our main research focus to this one?”

“There's no need for that!” the inspired Sub declared boldly. “We pledge to work on both tasks non-stop and in parallel!”

“You pledge?” the Queen asked with a hint of sly amusement.

“IN DEFENSE OF INTERESTS!” bellowed the well-drilled naturalists, snapping to attention on their lower steps.

“Well, splendid, if that's the case”, the Queen softened finally. “Tell your supervisor I'm satisfied for now”.

“IN DEFENSE!!! OF INTERESTS!!!” the lab crew croaked out even louder.

The Queen turned, went to the door, and exited. The scientists bowed as low as they could, their foreheads touching the staircase.

“It's a pity the dry water isn't ready yet”, she said musingly, as if making excuses when Rina caught up with her in the corridor. “But at least I'll have firing water, that's not bad either... Do they already produce combustible water on Earth?”

“No”, Rina replied. “We have carbonated water instead”.

“What do you mean, carb o nei ted?”

“It has gas bubbles”.

The Queen looked up at the ceiling and wondered if the bumbling lab techs could manage not two but all three tasks at once: dried water, burnable water, and now this one, with bubbles?

“No, they can't”, she decided, and strode resolutely toward the Western Tunnel.

At last, Rina reached the palace's underground levels. They were nothing like Earth's basements or catacombs – perfectly clean, well-ventilated, brightly lit rooms, though empty, with wide passageways, all crafted from the same cloud-like material, in the same style and with the same architectural details. The main way to distinguish the underground halls from the ballrooms upstairs was by their size, and above all, by the complete lack of luxury or any decoration at all.

There was no furniture either – instead of armchairs, tables, or sofas, the sub-palatial rooms were strewn with boxes of every imaginable shape, from mundane rectangular ones to egg-like or spiral ones, twisted like swirls of cream on a vanilla cake.

“I'm busy today, not a single spare instant”, the Queen lamented, “but regardless, I always find time to spend at the Fountain”.

25 – THE MORNING EVENING
part five

Rina felt a wave of nervousness in her soul. She knew that if everything went as Herx had predicted, she only had about half an hour left in the Air World. Time was now measured in minutes. She would never again see the Queen, or Princess Ahaa, or the beautiful white city of Ao. All of that would be in the past. There would be other lands, new people – and of course, new hardships.

“Will everything blow up again?” she wondered with a shiver.

Two forces were tearing at her. Yes, the thought of Nina haunted her, but Herx had told her that Nina was safe for now, it wasn't a matter of saving her from pain or suffering, but rather freeing her from captivity. Of course, the sooner Nina was free, the better, but again, Herx said Rina was moving through worlds rather quickly. That meant there was still time. That fact lessened her anxiety and gave her at least an hour or two of peace – to look around, to take everything in, and to remember.

She suddenly realized that she wasn't just leaving the Cloud World – she was leaving it FOR GOOD. No one would ever again let her wander on mist and steam, glide in transparent boats along airy rivers, or feast on pink clouds. No one would ever again permit her to enter the Palace of the Air Queen, to stroll the radiant, incomparable streets of the sky city, or ride a huge cloud zeppelin. None of these wonders would ever happen again.

And for a moment, a brief but palpable instant, Rina felt sorry to part with this strange, unusual, often puzzling, but kind and divinely magnificent world...

“Do you see the light?” Aoeina asked softly as she slowed her pace.

“Light?” Rina looked along the bright, snowy-colored corridor.

“The blue light of the Ice Fountain”.

Rina squinted, straining her eyes, but didn't see anything blue.

“Well, I see it”, said the Queen. “I can see it even from here. I think I could spot that light from ten thousand steps away... Listen”, the Queen stopped, and sensing the gravity of the moment, switched back to the formal royal contralto, “But you never told me your friend Herx had been to our world”.

“I don't know if he had or hadn't”, Rina replied honestly. “It's entirely possible he just knows about the worlds from his old books”.

“Are those books ancient, perhaps prehistoric?”

“I'm sure they are”.

“Impossible! The Fountain is young, it's only one hundred and five years old. That means Herx couldn't have read about it in such books. So it follows that your knight had been here personally. In our Palace, and in this very place”.

“So it seems”, Rina confirmed.

“No, it doesn't!” the Queen shot back. “We've kept a detailed record of everyone who's ever been allowed in here, every year, all through the century. I know that list by heart, and there's not a single Herx on it!”

“But Herx has many friends”, Rina tried, “and maybe one of them – an old buddy – told him about the Fountain and the Palace and everything. Some of his friends are here, in the Kingdom, like Aoao, for example”.

“I knew it!” the Queen exploded. “Of course! Who else could it be? Who else would betray the interests of the state if not that famous schemer and unbeatable blockhead – your Aoao! So those are the kinds of friends your Herx has! Strange, very strange...” The Queen stared directly into Rina's eyes.

Rina realized she had blurted out too much and was deathly afraid she would be denied access to the Fountain at the very last moment. Her thoughts spun madly, firing in a frantic search for any way to fix it.

“Your Majesty”, she said with her voice trembling, “please allow me to explain. Herx is a busy man, he has so many projects, so many people to meet – it's part of his job to deal with people. But if you know a lot of people, among them can be good ones and bad ones – anyone! Imagine you were friends with someone for twenty years, and then you find out this old friend of yours is actually a thief. He stole something recently. But you wouldn't say you never knew him, right? Maybe he was an honest person for years, and one day he just up and took something precious. And now people might say you were friends with a thief. But that's not fair! He was good and decent the day you first met him!”

The Queen stuck out her lower lip and thought deeply. A few moments later she smiled and exclaimed:

“You know what? You're right! Absolutely! I always knew how foolish and wrong it was to judge people by the company they keep. And you can't evaluate a person just by words either. If you need to know someone, find out what they do – go and see what they've accomplished in life, see it with your own eyes, and then everything will make sense. No other way! Aoao wasn't always a traitor either. Once, he was a wonderful person, I remember. He even helped the royal family in a difficult time, literally saved us from disaster, and of course we all saw him as a dear friend. But times change. People and circumstances alter too, and Aoao went bad... You know, you are right. Your famous knight Herx is not to blame for others' words or wickedness!”

Rina felt as if an incredibly heavy boulder lifted from her shoulders – such a sudden relief even made her dizzy for a moment.

“But we're here”, said the Queen, turning into a wide tunnel that soon led into a small polygonal chamber.

Many years ago, the Fountain stood proudly at the center of a specially-built platform, but after several particularly unfortunate renovations to the Palace – like many other underground relics – it had been unjustly shoved into a corner. Now it stood there in the dimness, lonely, plain, unremarkable, huddled against a cold wall. If not for the Queen, Rina probably wouldn't have found it.

At least, nothing in the rough gray basalt-like structure, barely five feet tall, suggested this was the one and only Blue Ice Fountain – a god-wrought wonder, a marvel of the world – once the spark of endless talk in the Palace and across the kingdom, a historic treasure immortalized in poems, novels, plays, and operas, and for over a century the subject of meticulous records detailing who had seen it, when, and why. Although, Herx claimed that no such lists truly existed – who knows, maybe he was wrong...

“Let's just stand here”, Aoeina suggested. “It doesn't like fuss”.

They stood a few steps away from the Fountain. The Queen leaned her shoulder against a cool column and closed her eyes. Three or four minutes passed in complete stillness and silence. Rina was ready to wait as long as it took. The only thing she wished for was a bench – or even a tiny stool. In the past hour, her weight had shifted again. Despite the recent cloudy dinner, her sneakers were sinking into the floor as if she were crossing a muddy spring field. Each new step took much effort, and now all Rina wanted was to quietly perch by a wall, in a corner, and sit there for a bit, relaxing her legs and not bothering anyone.

But soon she forgot about her aching knees – the Ice Fountain came to life! Unexpectedly, right from the small bowls a thick blueness poured silently, it was light, and yes – it poured, precisely the way rose oil would, or freshly extracted honey. Viscous streams of the purest light rolled over the edges of the bowls in long waves and flowed down, filling the shallow basin below with luminous liquid. Rina stepped closer.

“Careful!” warned the Queen. “Don't look too long – it might hurt your eyes”.

Rina glanced back – the Queen had squinted tightly, as if standing in front of a glaring lamp. And when Rina came right up to the Fountain, the Queen couldn't take it any longer and shielded her eyes with her hand. But Rina stared at the streams of wondrous light without any discomfort. She desired to touch the light with her fingers, to play with it, crumpling and pressing it somehow in her fist.

Despite the similarity of the glowing streams to honey, she understood it instantly – the Fountain had nothing to do with liquids. It was just light, natural light, compressed a million times over. This was the fulfillment of ancient dreams of citizens of the Cloud World. In that minute Rina understood why the Fountain was guarded so thoroughly.

“But what if I take some light in a bag?” a bold thought flashed through her mind.

She immediately asked the Queen for permission.

“That's never been done before”, said Aoeina in confusion. “We've been protecting the Ice Fountain for a hundred years – it's one of our greatest treasures”.

“Your Majesty, you protect the Fountain, not the light produced by the Fountain”, Rina gently insisted. “Nothing bad will happen if I take just a few grams to show the Blue Light in my world, on Earth. No one will believe me unless I bring at least a drop”.

“Oh, you've given me quite the puzzle, Earthling”. The Queen's brows went very high. “I don't think there's a solution to this riddle even in the Code of White Laws”.

Still, she allowed it. On the condition that Rina would collect the Light from the basin, not the bowls, and no more than three handfuls. Rina herself wasn't sure why she needed this light. She even thought that beyond the borders of the Fourth World, it might evaporate or transform into mist or ordinary water, just like the magical flower water from the Yellow Brook. However, despite the logic, she decisively pulled a plastic bag out of her backpack, and scooped some Blue Light with her bare hand. The light was intangible – absolute emptiness. Still, Rina had expected something like that.

“It's time I got back to my duties”, said the Queen once Rina had tucked the glowing little sphere into her backpack pocket. “If you like, you may stay here or walk through the lower passages. I permit it. And don't worry about getting lost – wherever you are, just shout ‘aae' – remember that, ‘aae' – and a watchman will find you at once and show you the way up”.

“Thank you, Your Majesty, I know the way”. Rina smiled. “If you don't mind, yes, I'll stay a little longer by the Fountain to remember it better, and a bit later I'll return... to the Throne Room”.

“Excellent. I won't be near the Throne, by the way, but I'll make arrangements and the Second Administrator Aotis will meet you. He'll take you to the guest rooms. So long. Until tomorrow”.

“Goodbye, Your Majesty”, said Rina with sadness.

But the Queen didn't hear the sorrow in her voice – she quickly walked back down the corridor, already absorbed in thinking about the potential uses and state-level applications of combustible water, if those lab dummies ever managed to produce it.

“So that's it. The journey through the Air World is coming to an end”, whispered Rina, watching the Queen go, and feeling her chest tighten from those words, her fingers going numb with emotion. “Okay. Fasten your seatbelts, stow tray tables. Cabin crew, prepare for landing”.

She returned to the Fountain, sat on the edge of the basin, and suddenly plunged both hands into the Blue Light up to her elbows. Then she scooped up a handful and brought it to her lips. She touched the light. And felt nothing.

“Yes, you're right, Your Majesty, it's time I got back to business!” she said louder. “Farewell, beautiful Cloud World!”

She looked up and shouted toward the high stone vaults:

“Wueot!”

A timid echo scurried through the corridors.

26 – KILLING FLOWERS
part one

“Wueot!” she called out loudly once more, and thought:

“How would Aoao even know I'm already here?”

But Aoao not only knew – he had been watching Rina's every step from the very moment she first entered the Palace. And although he had slipped from favor and had been demoted to the humiliating position of Assistant Watchman, many wise courtiers still maintained contact with him, well aware of their Great Queen's fickle and unpredictable nature.

“Only the Beyond knows”, they mused, “how this quarrel between the stubborn Advisor and the scatterbrained Ruler will end. It's quite possible Aoao will be exiled to the distant islands. But just as likely, he could become High Minister by tomorrow”.

And so, palace folk continued to relay to him through their servants all the most secret, important, or remotely significant royal news – so much so that in his current position Aoao was sometimes even better informed than he had been when he served as the Queen's Senior Advisor.

A few days earlier, Aoao, out of boredom, had traveled in a controlled dream, passing from there into the Mid-Space, where he encountered Herx. They were old acquaintances, close in their own cautious way – Herx had occasionally helped Aoao in affairs useful to the Near Worlds and had even pulled him out of tight spots a few times. And so, when the wizard, after the usual conversations about troubles, business, family, and politics, asked him about Rina, Aoao agreed without hesitation...

“Wueot! Wueot!! Wueot!!!” Rina shouted at the top of her lungs.

“Highest Heavens!” exclaimed Aoao behind her.

Rina jumped up as if she had springs in her feet – for a moment, her heart plummeted into Hades.

“You're only supposed to say it once! Once! And quietly – whispering!” Aoao said in dismay. “There are always dozens of spies in the Palace, eager to eavesdrop on my secrets!” But noticing the terror in her eyes, he softened and bowed. “At your service, Assistant Watchman of the White Tunnels, Aeon Aoao”.

Rina waited a few seconds until the shock faded to a tolerable level, exhaled, and said, “I'm Rina”, offering her hand.

Aoao bowed again, but following local custom, he didn't shake her hand – instead, he touched it lightly with the tips of his fingers.

“Very pleased... Herx asked me recently, and I will help you reach the Third Entrance to the next Near World. But first, you must answer one question directly”.

“What question?”

Aoao scratched his ear.

“Well... do you... are you afraid of dogs?”

“Dogs?! You have dogs?!”

As far as she could tell, there wasn't a single animal in the entire Air Kingdom.

“You're right”, Aoao agreed. “We have no animals here, neither wild nor domestic. Like in the other sky worlds, animals are forbidden in the Air Realms. But we do have flowers”.

“Ae?”

“Exactly – Ae”, Aoao nodded.

“So what does that have to do with dogs?”

“You see, Ae – we only call it a flower because it looks like some flowers from solid planets, but it's not really a plant. Some of the Ae species behave almost like your Earthly dogs – sometimes cheerful and silly, sometimes funny, but often vicious, even rabid. In short, be extremely careful with them”.

“I don't need flowers or dogs. I'm looking for the Entrance”.

“Of course, of course”, Aoao agreed calmly. “The Entrance is right there, in the Ae plantations”.

“In the dog-flower plantations?”

“Uh-huh”, Aoao nodded.

Silence followed. Finally, the watchman said:

“All right, now knock on the wall”.

“The wall?” Rina was clueless.

“So Herx never explained how to get to the Ae?”

“He said you would tell me everything”.

“Very strange”. The watchman shrugged. “Well, of course I'll tell you... It's not difficult, really. Go up to the wall and pound on it with your fists as hard as you can. Make some noise. The footmen and lackeys will come running to the sound, and then you yell, ‘Cave-in! Cave-in!' Do your best to create maximum panic. That's all”.

“What does ‘cave-in' mean?”

“It means what I'll make happen. I have stone traps set up. As soon as everyone rushes over and starts shouting, I'll trigger a collapse, a rockfall – just a fake one. No one will get hurt, but the tunnels will close temporarily – they're built that way. Then the footmen will panic and run to the secret Second Path. Unfortunately, only initiates and their servants can open the passage to the Second Path – no one else. Otherwise, we wouldn't need this whole performance. By the way, don't wait for an invitation from the servants – they won't have time for you. So when they bolt toward that wall – see over there, at the fork? The white wall with the blue top? – run with them immediately. And shout something silly and do not forget to elbow them hard”.

“Elbow them? But why?”

“So the servants think you're one of them. Camouflage. You understand?”

“And then?”

“Then it's easy. The path goes through the Black Hall. It's small and utterly black indeed, you won't mistake it. Just touch the floor – if it's warm, even hot, you're in the correct spot. Then you must fall behind the crowd and go to the right wall. Right – remember that. There's a door with a long handle – open it, step in and hide. When the noise dies down, come out and look for a niche on the opposite wall. Stand in it facing the door and wait. Two sidewalls will dissolve – go into the left one”.

Rina listened, mentally drawing a map, path, hall, right, door, in, out, niche, in, left.

“Would be nice not to get lost”. She worried.

Aoao continued:

“Inside, walk ten steps forward, and stop. First, everything vanishes, then the plantation, the Ae-2 garden, appears. Try not to speak to the flowers, don't do a thing for them. They'll beg, plead, flatter, call you their mother, queen, and savior. Remember – it's all lies. The real Ae can't talk. Go and find it there. The Ae will be alone on a special platform. And it's large. Then look carefully around it. Somewhere near, there should be a black cube, like a little box. Put it in your right pocket – the right one! That's all”.

“And the Entrance will open on its own? Automatically?”

“Yes, the Entrance will open – though not automatically, but magastically, as we say. Well, technologically, in your terms”. Aoao smiled. “Did you remember everything?”

“I hope I did”, Rina answered uncertainly.

“You know”. Aoao peered closely at Rina's face. “This is the seventh time I've met Earthlings in our kingdom, and you're all so different that sometimes I think you come from various worlds. Maybe it's because your domain is so complex, or the planet so vast – hard to tell”.

“Air people aren't all the same either”, Rina replied. “Take you, for instance – you're helping me, others aren't”.

“The others know nothing about you”.

“That's not why”. Rina looked into Aoao's blue eyes. “It's just that some people are good, and others are... indifferent. That's what I think”.

Aoao didn't reply. He walked over to the wall and said:

“Knock here. This spot will probably make the loudest sound. Now I must go, we shouldn't be seen together”.

“Goodbye. Thank you so much”.

“Goodbye”, said Aoao, offering his hand.

Rina touched his palm with one finger and smiled. The watchman turned to leave, but she suddenly remembered:

“Aunissa!” she exclaimed.

“Aunissa?” Aoao looked thrown.

“Yes! Aunissa. She asked me to tell you that the ants remember you and are very grateful for your help and kindness”.

The Advisor turned pale, lowered his head, and after a pause, quietly asked:

“How is she?”

“She's doing great!” Rina said joyfully. “They recently managed to repair the Yellow Brook, and now there are balls and festivals in the capital”.

“Festivals...” Aoao echoed.

But he quickly pulled himself together and said:

“Yes, don't forget, please, first the hot floor, then to the right wall, the door, then the niche opposite, from there to the left. Left, not right! That's important! Then ten steps and the black cube under the big flower... That's all. Farewell!”

“Farewell”.

The Advisor left. Rina was left alone.

“Yeah... I delivered the royal greeting from another world to him, and he... He could have at least said thank you... They're really strange here, I don't know...”

She stood, thinking. But there was no time for moral reflections or pondering customs and manners of the Air World. She had to act.

“So, that wall over there, then the hall, hot floor, door to the right, the handle, the niche opposite, wall to the left...” she mumbled, committing the instructions to memory.

Within a minute, she was ready.

“Ba-bang! Ba-bang! Ba-bang!” she banged both fists against the thin, echoing wall.

“Vva-Vvannn! Vva-Vvannn! Vva-Vvannn!” howled the echo through the passages.

She paused, listened, then renewed her pounding with fresh force:

“BA-BANG!!! BA-BANG!!! BA-BANG!!!”

Very soon, quick footsteps slapped through the tunnels, distant doors creaked open, voices shouted, and a few moments later, four servants in blue liveries of the secondary staff burst into the hall.

“What's going on? What's happening?” the servants asked, barely stopping.

“Cave-in! Cave-in!” Rina screamed in a theatrical, panicked voice.

“Cave-in! Cave-in!” the servants shrieked, also in a panic – but a real one.

A second later, a terrifying stampede sounded, and a whole pack of blue-uniformed lackeys flew around the corner and slammed at full speed into the shouting group. Now everyone was bellowing and roaring, mercilessly tearing their lungs to pieces – faces wild, arms flailing, all thrashing about the square, shoving each other. More servants ran in from every direction, and the crowd swelled in no time.

And then – a monstrous crash! The floor twitched madly, as if from an underground explosion. In unthinkable confusion, with savage shrieks, the servants bolted toward the exit. But enormous stones rolled toward them, having broken loose from the ceiling.

“Treason!” one crazed footman yelled right in Rina's face. “Treason! Run! Run! Save yourselves!”

“Why treason?” a quick thought flickered. But there was no time to figure it out – the servants didn't wait.

In an instant, the frenzied, maddened horde stormed back across the hall, and crashed into the angular white wall like a heavy wave smashing into a cliff. Rina fought to keep up, but the softening floors slowed her down and hampered every step. Under the huge impact of the human mass, the wall gave way, buckled, bent, and then burst into sharp fragments like thin glass. The servants, never stopping, rushed into the revealed, unlit corridor.

At last, with great effort, Rina managed to catch up to a pair of runners. Just in case, following the watchman's advice, she elbowed one of them rather harshly – but she was instantly paid back with a perfectly trained, mighty kick to her thigh, so she decided not to experiment with local traditions any further.

Fortunately, after about another hundred yards, the dark tunnel opened into a gloom-shrouded, murky room poorly lit by a pale-blue globe overhead. Rina pretended to stumble, crouched quickly, and touched the floor – it was as hot as a skillet!

“Black Hall!” She rejoiced.

There was no need to hide – the servants raced on ahead, already out of sight, and soon silence returned to the room. The door with the so-called “long handle” was right there – a thin, white grip that looked more like a machine lever. Rina tried to open the door, but the lever struck a stone ledge – the handle needed to be pushed down somehow first. She did just that – the lever slid inward and the door opened easily.

Well, it worked, but behind the door loomed an empty space about ten feet deep and nothing else.

“Seriously? They want me sitting in this closet? Why?” Rina felt a bit irritated.

However, after all her travels through the worlds, she had learned that the most rational course was always to follow directions. And though there was not a thing behind the door, and the room looked pretty pointless, Rina stepped inside and stood there for half a minute. Just in case. Then she came out, carefully shut the door, returned the lever to its original position, and hurried to the niche she had noticed earlier. She knew how to use niches, there were many of them on Ahaa's ship. But tunnel niches worked on a slightly different principle.

She stepped into it and waited, eyes fixed on the wall. One minute, two. Nothing happened.

“It would be awful if this device broke”, she said aloud worriedly. “I have no way of calling tech support out here”.

Another minute passed – still nothing. Rina decided to exit and repeat the process. She stepped out and back in again. No! Nothing had changed! Not in the slightest.

Despair crept in. Frantic thoughts raced through her mind, maybe the system malfunctioned because of the artificial rockfall? Or maybe Aoao had betrayed her? Though she couldn't believe that.

Suddenly, somewhere behind, deep in the tunnel, voices echoed. Several people were hurriedly approaching the Black Hall from the palace side.

“...yes, that's right, but don't worry, My Lord, we'll be done by morning, one night is more than enough for that”, said a clear voice.

“If I were sure of you”, a cranky, elderly voice retorted, “I'd have sent my secretary here instead of crawling around in these stinking holes”.

“I'm doomed...” Rina's blood ran cold.

Maybe in moments like these, the mind works ten times faster, in an instant, Rina replayed her entire conversation with Aoao and... she found it!

“Facing the door! Facing the door!” she whispered excitedly.

In one move she spun around to face the door with the white handle and stopped breathing. Right then both the walls began quivering, paling, and dissolving into the air.

The voices were growing louder. It seemed the fading of the walls and the nearness of the people were physically linked – the more transparent the walls became, the louder the voices. But the transfer portal, because that's what it really was, activated one beat sooner. At that instant His Excellency the First Senior Architect stepped into the Black Hall, Rina was already running full speed down a narrow left passageway at the southern edge of the White Island. The palace dungeons lay a dozen miles behind.

She might have gone much farther than was needed, but as soon as she stopped, the corridor lights went out. And when they came back on, the gray walls were no more. All around her was a cute spring garden filled with enormous flowers up to her chest.

26 – KILLING FLOWERS
part two

From a distance, the flowers resembled tulips, though they were far larger than their cousins in Holland. And the buds, as if made of rubber, stretched slightly upwards. Thin sharp petals, pale-pink in color, olive leaves, bluish stalks. But Rina noticed that everything was too even, too flat, painted too perfectly. Honestly, the garden seemed artificial – cheap plastic toys. However, as Rina came closer, she realized the plants were not just alive and bouncy – they were active and vigorous.

Still, Ae was not a living thing, it was handmade anyway. Though capable of thought. Essentially, Ae was the highest form of existence of cloud materials. The Air World had two such high-level formations, one 80% natural, another 80% synthetic. The latter was a great creation by students of the Royal Cloud Academy.

The original Ae, actually Ae-1, had moderate intelligence, its own “self”, and could act independently, though not always rationally, in relation to humans. People considered Ae-1 a new race, once conveniently seen as something like domestic pets – though true animals couldn't exist in the Air World due to the altered physics of the air space and strict ancient prohibitions, as Aoao had said earlier.

The synthetically engineered Ae-2, made in labs from the same remarkable thinking substance, looked almost identical to the original, but was nothing more than a crude, laughable parody. It babbled about random topics without understanding anything, fawned, groveled, begged for help and sympathy, but at the first opportunity, using its petals, it would try to slice off its owner's head with great pleasure.

Fortunately, as a spoof, it was a ridiculous caricature in every respect. Its petals were fragile and soft, and the flower lacked the strength to even swat a mosquito – if mosquitoes existed there. Its violent floral bloodlust provoked only scornful laughter from people, and among the flowers it caused nervous breakdowns and deep inferiority complexes.

Still, thanks to poor memory, within a week the Ae-2 would completely forget the insult and begin hatching new vicious schemes, eagerly dreaming of cutting the throat of the gardener who cared for him.

“Lovely lady!” the nearest flower shouted. (It is worth noting that Ae-2 creations had poor eyesight from birth – they could see no farther than five paces away.) “My true queen! I love you and worship you! I am ready to give my miserable life for you – every last drop of my blood!”

Rina recoiled in fear, but soon understood that the flowers could not walk, and couldn't cause any real harm, so she just tried to pass them and head for the exit, pretending to notice nothing. But at that moment, the next flower across the path awoke and immediately began to wail with a dramatic flair:

“Ah! Look, my brothers! The Great Savior has come to us, our deliverer from the cruel forces of darkness and liberator from our ignorant, evil tormentors! She will protect us, she will not allow the beautiful, delicate flowers to perish in the ruthless hands of merciless cannibals!”

“Cannibals?” Rina repeated incautiously.

“Cannibals! Cannibals! Real cannibals!” the flowers shrieked from all sides.

“In this sorrowful land, all gardens are ruled by murderers”, lamented another Ae-2. “Their filthy hands are soaked in the innocent blood of defenseless little flowers! Only a kind, wonderful angel like you can help us! Save us, please save us!”

The flowers wept, groaned, sobbed – huge drops of liquid mist rolled down their long, grief-stricken faces. Rina was bewildered, flustered, not knowing what to think.

“Help us!” the flowers pleaded, caught up in a passionate, theatrical ecstasy. “Only you! You are the one! We rely on you alone! At least save our children!”

“But what am I supposed to do?” she asked, completely disoriented.

At that very second, the weeping and wailing stopped. The flowers clearly hadn't expected this turn of events.

“How can I help you?” Rina asked again.

“Uhh... well... you see... we... here... we're... flowers...” one of the previously tearful petitioners stammered.

“We're here, we're flowers!” the boldest Ae repeated loudly. All the rest turned their pink heads toward him in hope. “We're suffering and dying from the violence and injustice of the gardeners!”

“But what injustice?” Rina missed the point. “Tell me, I'll talk to your gardener and settle everything. I'm good at settling things”.

“You see”, the boldest flower continued, now even more confidently, “our gardeners are stupid. They can't manage one simple yet absolutely imperative task”. The Ae-2 felt a surge of deceitful energy and, to his own surprise, struck upon a brilliant idea. “The gardeners can't provide one critically, crucially important condition”, he fabricated, increasingly inspired. “I'd even say a vital one”.

“But what condition? Tell me”, Rina asked, now completely off guard.

“To be smelled!” Ae-2 blurted out. The surrounding flowers gasped in awe. “We need to be smelled!” he continued, “because we are flowers!”

“To be smelled! To be smelled! We are flowers!” the Ae-2s shouted almost in unison, thrilled by the exceptional elegance of the deceit.

“Gardeners, as everyone knows, are crude and uneducated”, the flower proclaimed with feeling. “Primitive creatures, all they want is to laze in hammocks and pig out on sour fog. They are never concerned about the spiritual suffering of the poor flowers!”

“They are cruel! Down with the gardeners!” came cries from the crowd.

“So, what you want is... for me to just smell you all?” Rina tried to clarify the idea.

“Exactly! Just smell us! Smell us all! Nothing more!”

Rina shrugged with a smile.

“Well, if that really helps you, I'm in. I can do it. Doesn't bother me. How many of you are there?”

“Twenty-eight in total, but we won't count the three little ones”.

“All right. Who's first?”

“Me!!!” all twenty-eight shouted at once.

Rina glanced around to make sure no one was watching the ridiculous scene, then she leaned toward the first flower.

The instant her face came closer, the first Ae-2, without hesitation, slashed at Rina's neck with a long curved leaf in a lightning-fast movement. The thin leaf snapped and crumbled to pieces.

“Ow!” Rina jumped back. “What are you doing?!”

“I'll kill you!!!” the flower screamed.

“Kill her! Kill her!” the rest took up the chant.

Rina ran. Though the flimsy leaf hadn't even left a mark on her skin, she was scared to death. She fled through the flowerbed, as the long-faced Ae-2s reached for her, trying to grab even just her clothing.

Soon, the plantation was behind her. Rina burst into a wide green-and-blue clearing, brightly lit by a huge white orb hanging high under the dome's ceiling. In the middle of the vast, empty space stood a lone Ae, looking similar to the Ae-2s though about twice as tall and with a much pinker head.

“They. Wanted. To kill me!” Rina exclaimed, trembling with rage and horror. “What... scoundrels!” she struggled to find the right word. “They need to be weeded! Like nettles! And doused in... in... chemicals! Immediately!”

But after calming down a bit, she thought:

“Still... I can't believe how easily they fooled me...”

Meanwhile, the real Ae was straining to listen to the continuing uproar coming from the plantation and kept his eyes fixed on Rina. He not only couldn't speak, but barely moved his leaves, and now, to make things worse, his upper petal had pressed against his ear opening and couldn't be shaken off.

Rina immediately noticed the little black cube glinting with its polished edges – it was slightly larger than a matchbox – lying on a flat stone half a meter from the flower. But how to take it? Who knew what Ae-1 was capable of?

She looked around for something to defend herself with. In the right corner of the yard, some construction had been left unfinished – tools and wall debris were scattered on the ground. Rina approached and picked up a long instrument that looked somewhat like a railroad hammer, though it was light and flexible. Nevertheless, just holding it in her hand made her feel much safer.

“Just try it! I dare you! You'll get it!” she said loudly to Ae, shaking her “weapon”.

But Ae seemed too preoccupied with the noise still echoing from the overly agitated plantation and now showed open indifference toward the stranger. And of course, this only deepened Rina's suspicions. She gripped the “hammer” tightly with both hands, ready to fend off any attack – or so she thought. A step, another, three steps more. The box was close.

“I'll go around from behind”, she whispered, circling the flower.

“Uhhhhh”, Ae moaned quietly.

Rina tensed and aimed her “halberd” exactly at his head, but the flower stood still, rooted in place.

“Oh, what can this stupid daisy do to me, anyway?” she thought scornfully, with tired annoyance. She stepped forward decisively, bent down, and grabbed the box.

The slick black metal radiated unbearable cold, searing her fingers. Rina threw the weapon aside and dashed to the wall, tossing the box from hand to hand.

“I need to wrap it in something. If I carry it in my pocket, it'll freeze me in no time. And why the pocket anyway, why not the backpack?”

She pulled out a handkerchief and wrapped the cube, but then, remembering the exact “instructions”, she placed it in her jacket's side pocket. In the right one.

“Well, that's done. Now we wait for the magic”.

Of course, crossing into new worlds was nerve-wracking, but her mood had noticeably improved, and now the idea of a cosmic relocation didn't scare her any more than a flight from Kyiv to Naples: all that rush, hassle, stress, documents, checks, waiting, running, nerves, and nerves again – but the actual flight lasted a blink. You just sit down, buckle in, get comfy, sip some juice, drink some tea, crunch some nuts, gossip with Nina, and flip through the magazine – absolutely useless, nothing but perfume ads and watch posters – then you glance out the window – a bunch of colorless bald mountains, that's all. And right after that – there you are – “Prepare for landing”.

Whether the upcoming interplanetary passage was risky, whether it could fail or end in a catastrophe – no one knew. Only Herx could shed light on that business. But he hadn't mentioned any special danger. So, the trip should be effortless. Though again, Rina still kept shuddering when she recalled her transition to the Ocean World, where she endured the full horror of real drowning. And the nuclear-explosive route to the Air World – honestly, wasn't any better...

“Well, after all, ice and snow are just winter – not exactly extreme, like the ocean”, she thought. Then she remembered she had nothing warm on her, aside from her thin denim jacket. “I hope I don't get dropped off on a glacier somewhere in Antarctica, a hundred miles from a polar station”.

Three minutes passed, but nothing happened – no secret doors appearing, no blaze and fire in the building, no snow falling from the ceiling – nothing interesting at all.

“Maybe I messed it up again”. She worried and took the box out.

Amazingly, it was now warm, almost hot – like a tungsten bulb turned off a minute ago. Rina removed the handkerchief and carefully, afraid of getting burned, held the cube to her ear as if listening to the ocean in a seashell. But no, no sound at all, the cube was silent as a stone.

“What if I tap it with my nail? Or shake it? Or just give it an order?”

She looked at the Ae. The flower seemed to be keeping a close eye on her with cunning glances – clearly spying. She decisively stepped toward him, showed him the cube from all sides and asked, smiling as kindly as she could:

“Excuse me, Sir, do you happen to know what it is ... I mean... how to use this thing?”

“Evvv”, Ae buzzed faintly.

“No, he doesn't know”. Rina sighed.

But right then something changed – the floor and walls jerked three inches to the side, then immediately snapped back. Like a real earthquake. Except Rina didn't move at all. The marble slab slipped out from under her sneakers for a second, then just as quickly slid back beneath them.

“Well, here we go”, she whispered nervously, clutching the gradually cooling box in her palm.

The space stirred again. A tremor ran beneath her feet. Then everything twitched violently and rebounded in an instant. Very soon, the jolts began coming one after another, without any rhythm, but their amplitude increased, the impulses turned into a heavy shuddering, then furious shaking, and after a couple of minutes, all the surrounding objects, vibrating frenziedly, were no longer discernible. Everything started jumping madly and uncontrollably, and lost its form. Even the Ae flower blurred into a smear of multicolored stripes and blotches, swirling together like in a blender.

Rina hovered in emptiness, detached from the crazed space. She noticed that the jumps and convulsions were gradually gaining force, speeding up toward the right side, spiraling into some sort of vortex. Dashes, splashes, and blurs raced and flickered in wild leaps, overtaking one another mid-motion. A sort of violently quivering funnel, with her – Rina – exactly at its center, held there by something unknown.

“Great, I'm clearly about to begin falling again”, flashed through her panicked mind.

Soon, the vortex stretched up and down, extending hundreds of yards above and below – at least that's how it looked from the inside – and before long, everything morphed into a colossal, rapidly spinning vertical tunnel that seemed to reach into the cosmic heights. Rina felt waves of warm wind pressing up from below. She wobbled, let out a cry, stretched her arms to balance, and... she flew. Powerful, firm currents caught her and carried her upward, as if an invisible giant were blowing on her, toying with her, the way children blow on dandelion fluff, trying to keep it afloat in the air.

WORLD FIVE – BUTTERFLIES
27 – DINISSA'S BLUE MEADOWS
part one

Above and below, no matter how hard she looked, there was only the same colorful spinning tunnel stretching into both infinities – and nothing else. Rina floated in the air quite effortlessly, like a feather, but couldn't gauge her speed – not by the wind, nor by the blurred streaks and smears rotating around. Her eyes couldn't focus, and it was practically impossible to tell the size of those stripes – maybe twenty feet, maybe half a mile. Many times she tried to flip over, to a more comfortable position, but the stubborn air stream nudged her back.

“Dang! They're sending me to the Fifth World like a package through pneumatic tubes”, she said indignantly to the imaginary “owners” of the tunnel. After a moment's thought, she decided to pull out the map – she had nothing better to do anyway. The flight, clearly, wasn't going to be short. Not that she was notably worried about what lay at the end of this painted tube.

“Most likely I'll crash into a throne room of some local ice prince”, she thought. “It's okay as long as they give me a fur coat. And warm boots – that's what really matters!”

The map was black like a moonless night in November. Rina searched through every inch, but found nothing except a few specks of sand.

“All right. The map only shows worlds, but between them it shuts off”, she correctly deduced. With a sigh, Rina slipped her most valuable treasure back into her pocket and grew bored.

“Igor's birthday is today”, she remembered, thinking back over the last week. “I would've given him a book. Probably Aneshka Vitte's ‘The Ninth War'. It's a good story, though for real, I'm starting to grow out of that kind of robo-fantasy”.

She imagined herself at Igor's apartment, a small cake, three boxes of pepperoni pizza and one vegetarian – for her and Svetka – then half a dozen bottles of Coca-Cola, one large Sprite, and maybe even non-alcoholic champagne, why not? And as always, under the table – a mess of still unopened gifts, and high above – a garland of sports team flags. Igor is a huge football fan. And something droningly tedious in the air, echoing across the whole room – an ancient Pink Floyd album, his favorite ‘Dark Side'. Then Igor's mom – a noisy, red-faced woman always in black – shows pictures of their trip to the famous Dr. Vike in Kharkiv.

“No!” Rina corrected herself. “That's not her, that's Ghenka's mother who admires Dr. Vike and says he is a medical sorcerer. Igor's mom used to be a violinist. Or a violist. I don't know the difference”.

A faint echo rolled through the tunnel, like a distant rumble of thunder.

“What was that?”

But everything went quiet again – the flight continued on its set course – as TV reporters say. Though in real life, it was just wind blowing in the center of the pipe. An endless pipe, with air waves streaming from below – nothing more than that.

“Interplanetary ventilation”. Rina smirked and thought maybe it was nice to be a violinist – or even a violist. Your whole life, you play music for pleasure, and people listen, applaud, give you flowers, offer you awards, money, compliments, and fly you around the world for free – while you do nothing. You just play your violin. Not bad!

“They never signed me up for music school”, she said reproachfully.

Then she remembered it wasn't totally true. There had been one red-bearded, maroon-haired fellow they visited for an audition. He ran a famous children's choir – not a Ukrainian one though, it was from Central Asia, but that didn't matter – they often toured in Kyiv too. Every year the choir traveled abroad from place to place for several months, and each time, when they returned home, all the children automatically received good grades. That's what Nadya, her mom's friend, said. She'd given them the man's number. But the redhead didn't enroll Rina anyway, not even as a favor.

“I need pure sopranos, sweetheart”. He smiled, leaning toward her ear. “And you, my dear, you don't quite reach”.

“I don't quite reach!” Rina flared up, recalling that little slight. “If only you all knew where I am now! I'd love to see how you would manage on my journey – whether you'd ‘quite reach' or not!”

But sincerely, “all hands on my heart” – as Aunt Vera would joke – Rina didn't like the violin or choir or any other musical matters. More than disliked – she detested them. She never cared for sports or sculpting either. She dropped out of the art class after the first week and deliberately showed up late to gymnastics until they “politely” asked her to withdraw. No, Rina wasn't lazy – she just felt wired for something different, something she hadn't figured out yet.

“Vvuuu!”

A heavy bass sound swept through the tunnel.

“So, it's soon. If it hums again, I'll brace for landing”.

The prospect of landing in the World of Ice wasn't exactly a cheerful one. Winter was never her favorite season. Frost, especially with wind, irritated her skin, producing something almost like eczema. She had to coat her face and hands in an awful greasy ointment, sticky and disgusting, and then walk around all day long with a shiny nose – a nightmare! So if others had winter memories of sleds, snowmen, skates, and skis, hers and Nina's were all about medical lotions, prescriptions, and frozen cheeks.

Finally, this dreary flight had become unbearable. She couldn't stand looking at the flickering blots anymore – her eyes hurt, and craning her neck every time to see ahead was just annoying. Winter or not, this spinning pipe was the worst.

And then it happened... no, not a miracle. A primitive technical trick – the tunnel didn't dissolve, or dissipate, or evaporate. No, it just ended. The rotating tube cut off clean, like it had never existed. Rina was carried forward by momentum, now in total emptiness. Well, in air, more precisely.

And the air was filled with a delicious scent of honey. Very strange. Honey? Or floral? Or floral-with-a-kitchen-note? Hard to say. And she immediately noticed a grayish shape far above – maybe a cloud, or perhaps a patch of misty sky.

“What? Looks like I'm being accidentally rerouted back to the Cloud World”, she thought. But very soon she realized that she was plummeting – at an insane speed – toward the ground!

A land, covered in multicolored patches of meadows, forests, and lakes, surged down toward her like a massive wave. In all directions stretched an endless solid plain.

The ground! The firm, inevitable, fatal ground!

“I'm going to die!” she whispered in horror.

Panic overwhelmed her. But what worried her most was why the ground was above, not below?

The surface kept zooming in at a crazy pace, the speed increased, and wind howled in her ears like mad. She opened her mouth for a moment and a heavy gust slammed into her throat. Now she was too terrified to even breathe normally, let alone scream.

“This can't be happening! Can't! Be! Can't! Be!” pounded in her head.

She couldn't hold it in anymore, gasped for air and shrieked her head off.

“Hheerrx!”

The counterflow, hardened under pressure, nearly ruptured her lungs. Desperately trembling, she stared at the quickly growing meadows, rivers, and groves. Her brain refused to believe that in just a few seconds, the nice girl named Rina would turn into a bloody lump of dirt in the middle of a swampy grassland.

“Hheeerrrx!”

She screamed, shielding her face from the wind.

“Hheeeerrrrx!”

Whether her call had really worked, or that was how the landing sequence had been designed initially – or whatever the local engineers had cooked up – she didn't even manage to finish calling the wizard for the third time before she felt the braking motion, very familiar from her fall off the cloud-zeppelin.

And at that very moment, suddenly, instantly, and absolutely unexpectedly, the panic vanished. Rina composed herself with eerie ease, her emotions clicked back into their regular, comfortable positions.

“Are you kidding me?” she asked no one in particular.

The phrase sounded indignant but without a trace of worry.

“How did you come up with the idea of making a simple interworld transfer into a deadly fall without a parachute? What kind of psycho designed this?”

Rina figured that the most proper and technically flawless flight so far had been the one in the pea field.

“Though of course, that transition was done by Herx, and all the others were made by strangers”.

“Aliens”, she added mentally.

Meanwhile, the monstrous jump was smoothly turning into graceful gliding. Rina thought that maybe she wasn't so wrong when she jokingly called herself a tourist. Sliding along the warm currents above a beautiful green dale, she truly felt like a visitor being shown unique wonders of exotic nature from a bird's-eye view by the native chiefs – as if from a sightseeing helicopter. Only there was no helicopter, and no chieftains in sight either. Everything, as astronauts say, was happening in autonomous mode.

And below, there really was something to look at. It has long been known that from up high, even just from five hundred meters, any landscape looks photogenic and appealing, whether it's a dead, scorched desert, or lifeless bare cliffs, or even icebergs and icy plains.

Speaking of ice, Rina squinted at the earth pressing upward like a wall and saw all the signs of a sparkling southern spring, a hot sunny May. Everything was in bloom, the whole world flooded with light, a playful breeze chasing ripples across the silver saucers of little lakes, white clouds, blue skies – spring in full swing. And not a single hint of snow, ice, or frost.

Definitely, it's not easy to stay fully focused while falling from several miles up, especially when no one knows how the landing will actually happen, but still, the picture Rina saw below in detail was very much… Earth-like. Was it really Earth? But why?! That would be a true catastrophe. Earth would force her into a new trip through all the worlds again! It would reset the clock – back to the desert, the air, the ocean. All from scratch? No! Nightmare!

Although, experienced in travel, Rina decided to wait, to postpone worries. Better to be patient for a minute or so. The Near Worlds are complicated and extremely crafty things. What looks green and warm at first glance might very well turn out to be white and snowy on closer inspection.

To the ground – half a mile. Or less. Hard to calculate from above. Directly below her – an enormous flower-spotted hayfield. Or a meadow. Now Rina was falling at the speed of a snowflake, and was studying the alien landscape with great interest. But she saw no trace of a city, settlement, or even a small village. No civilization.

“People don't live here”, she concluded confidently.

Then who does? Not animals either, that's for sure... Though why not animals? The World of Ice... but wait, what nonsense – there's no sign of any ice! So where is it, all that promised snow, where's your blizzard, where are the white mountains? Gone!

To her great relief, just half a minute later the flight successfully ended. Rina was gently set down onto the soft grassy carpet. Thank you for flying our airline! And don't forget your belongings. Wow! The incomparable jump from the stratosphere (as newspapers used to write in large red headlines) was completed as planned – something Rina could congratulate herself for. And applaud, if she felt like it.

Standing for a while and taking a look around, she noticed a multitude of vividly colored butterflies living in the field. Honestly, she had never seen so many butterflies gathered in one place before. It looked like these flying insects had come together for a butterfly festival or maybe their beauty contest. Light-winged creatures of every color and size sat on every leaf, every stem. And three times as many flitted about in the air. Living flowers on the field, flying flowers in the sky – it was like something out of a Disney movie. What a pity she didn't have a video camera...

“Well, getting out of here without stepping on you is going to be tricky”, said Rina to the beauties assembled around her. They responded to the remark with a friendly fluttering of wings.

But she had to get out somehow. Rina tried shooing them away with her hands. No, it didn't work, only the closest ones noticed that. Then she plucked a long reed and, waving it over the sedge, thus lifting little clouds of butterflies, moved with all possible care toward the forest – and in about fifteen minutes was already under the pines. The forest was cool and calm, the tall reddish trunks gave off a sweet scent of amber resin. No butterflies. No paths either.

“Let's see what the map says!” she announced to the trees in a businesslike tone and unfolded her precious masterpiece on the layer of last year's pine needles.

The map astonished her, the entire piece, from edge to edge, was covered in hundreds of tiny markings of all sorts – little stars of different shades and shapes, microscopic pyramids, dots, symbols, signs, icons, unreadable labels, figures, and even pea-sized, ancient-looking faces. The little “Rina” – a girl in a skirt – was nowhere to be found. After half an hour of studying the local geography, Rina was entirely disoriented and had no idea what part of the world she was in or where she should go. She stared at the map, blinking in frustration, and could not decide which was better – to get angry or to take offense.

“Butterflies! It's the World of Butterflies! A planet of flying bugs! A whole universe of dumb, thickheaded insects!”

Yes, you guessed it – she decided to get a little angry. Getting angry was easier, after all. No, she tried taking offense too, but sulking when you are alone never lasts long. No point – nobody's watching. Besides, to be honest, Rina was actually pretty glad that butterflies had replaced ice and snow. Say what you will, but sun-loving, colorful cuties in a blooming meadow are still more appealing than even the most luxurious and super-wonderful snowstorm.

Since it was impossible to find the landing spot on the map, Rina chose to go back to the grassland and examine it more closely. Maybe she'd missed something – perhaps there was a sign, or a note, or any other clue that would point the way. One thing that kept bothering her was the butterflies. Insects are insects, silent, skittish, and unintelligible. It was unlikely she'd get anything out of them. Sure, some butterflies might be as chatty as the fish in the Ocean, but you wouldn't know it at first sight.

Unfortunately, even after the second, third, and fourth inspection, the butterflies didn't manage to change Rina's opinion. As they should, they behaved... like butterflies – flitting away at the slightest hint of danger, circling high above the flowers, skillfully catching tiny air whirlwinds in their wings. That's it.

“Useless. Use-leee-ss!” Rina said loudly, sitting down on the grass, plucking a big daisy, and smelling it. “You can't get anything out of them. They're not magical!”

In the end, only one thing remained – to go in the direction of the morning sun, meaning east. Just like it was in the Desert. No more precise landmarks had presented themselves. So, she went. Toward the sun was back to the forest. But the deeper she went into the pine thicket, the more impassable the undergrowth became. The endless timberland, so beautiful from afar, turned out to be totally wild within.

“Well, that's enough. You may consider me lost!” she said bitterly to the sky after about twenty minutes of senseless walking. “There's no way I'll find the butterfly meadow now”.

The forest felt unnatural, all the pines were too identical, as if they'd been planted on the same day a hundred years ago. But then she remembered Aunt Vera, who had told her during her previous visit that something like this happens in Canada after a massive fire, when the spruce trees, dried out by a rainless summer, burn down to the ground, and a new forest starts from the roots, from tiny shoots that break through the thick layer of last year's ash the following spring.

There was still a faint hope of stumbling upon a trail, even an old, overgrown one that hadn't been walked on in ages. Any path, regardless of its condition, might lead to... To what? To people? Do people even live in the Butterfly World?

“But if there's no one here except trees and butterflies, then where am I even going?” Rina stopped in confusion.

Yes, it would have been much more reasonable to stay in the flower valley and just wait to see what would happen next.

“How stupid everything is”, she said irritably to the closest pine and sat down on a long moss-covered log. “Well then, what now?”

Something lightly brushed the tip of her ear. Rina whipped around, but saw only a large white butterfly fluttering nearby. The butterfly made a wide circle, flying close to the tree trunks, then, drawing nearer, seemed to start dancing in the air, bouncing up and down, hopping side to side, and suddenly folded its wings and dropped into the sparse grass.

Rina jumped up and stepped toward it, but instantly, the butterfly lifted off and hovered right in front of Rina's face, as if looking deeply eye to eye. Its mysterious behavior left no doubt – it was intelligent and was clearly trying to say something with its dance. But what exactly?

“Who are you?” Rina asked, holding out her open palm. The butterfly didn't hesitate and readily landed on her pinky, tickling it with her tiny, clinging legs.

Maybe she really was saying something, but human ears weren't meant to hear such subtle sounds. What to do? Though magic, even the tiniest bit of it, is still magic. The butterfly fluttered up and immediately landed on Rina's head, so she felt the little white stranger anchoring itself there with firm, tenacious movements.

“|Hello|”, whispered a purely ethereal voice, barely perceptible somewhere on the very edge of consciousness.

Startled, Rina spun around, nearly dislodging the butterfly, but of course, no one was behind, just trees.

“|Hello|”, the same voice repeated, a bit louder and more persistently.

It wasn't in Rina's ears, it sounded directly in her mind, just like the voices of ocean creatures, though somehow different, somehow more complicated. The mental sounds had colors and shapes – each word looked like distant plumes of smoke. And the smoke was warm and soft, Rina could even feel it on the skin of her forehead.

“Y-yes... hello”, Rina replied uncertainly.

“|My name is Dinissa|”, the butterfly whispered.

“Are you a queen?” Rina asked, expecting further encounters with lofty beings.

“|A queen?|” the butterfly said in great surprise. “|No! Of course not!|” she laughed. “|How could I be a queen? I'm a fairy.|”

At first, Rina was stunned and didn't believe it. A fairy – just a fictional character from movies and cartoons. Sure, Herx, for example, could be called a mage or, as some would say, a miracle worker, but that was just a figure of speech. In truth, he wasn't magical at all, he was just someone who knew more than others. At least, he insisted on that. But a fairy – if she truly was a fairy – belonged to a completely different, nonexistent universe, an imaginary one.

“|You're right,|” Dinissa whispered cheerfully. “|My universe doesn't exist, it's your imagination.|”

“So I'm dreaming?”

“|No, you're not asleep. You're imagining. And you're a guest with us.|”

“With us who?” Rina laughed quietly at the absurdity. “If everything around is my imagination and doesn't exist, then who am I, and who are you?”

“|Yes, we exist, but no, we don't,|” the butterfly said with a smile (somehow Rina felt her smile rather clearly). “|As long as you imagine, we live in you. And you live with us.|”

Rina understood that diving deeper into concepts like those was dangerous – it was the kind of thing that could drive someone mad. Not long ago, she had read a sci-fi novel about a man who struggled to prove to himself that he lived in reality, and in the end, overwhelmed by contradictions in his own mind, he lost it forever.

“If we don't exist, then the fairy's right – the butterfly world is my imagination”.

But the butterfly shook her head. Like with the smile, Rina could not see that motion, though she perceived it absolutely plainly. One thing was obvious – the butterfly, perched on her palm, could hear her thoughts. Moreover, she easily slipped into Rina's feelings, probably even her memory.

27 – DINISSA'S BLUE MEADOWS
part two

“Dear Fairy Dinissa”, Rina addressed the butterfly politely but a bit formally after a brief pause, “Could we possibly speak in our real forms?” Rina remembered her astonishing encounter with Aunissa, Queen of the Ants.

“|In real… forms?!|” the fairy didn't understand the phrase.

“Yes, in our human bodies”.

The butterfly fluttered her wings, swept swiftly through the last few days in Rina's mind, but didn't find a proper solution.

“|I'm not human,|” Dinissa finally whispered, almost inaudibly.

“Well, then become one. Just for half an hour”, Rina said in an innocent tone.

“|But that is never workable!|” the butterfly's voice seemed to tremble. Her words appeared as billowy blue clouds on a distant imaginary horizon, but then a virtual wind blew them away.

“|Not workable,|” Dinissa repeated sadly and fell silent.

Rina didn't pursue the hopeless topic and asked the most important question – how to leave the Fifth World and cross into the Sixth one?

“|There is no exit from here,|” Dinissa declared. “|As long as you are in the state of ditilia, all other worlds are inaccessible to you.|”

“Ditilia?” Rina echoed, a little scared.

“|Yes, but don't worry,|” Dinissa sang. “|Ditilia isn't dangerous and it will end soon. Meanwhile, I'd like to invite you to my favorite Blue Meadows.|”
